## management-governance — module synthesis

_Source: `99_management-governance-synthesis.md`_

# Management-Governance Module — NHY (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Norsk Hydro's leadership reads as competent operators with only moderate alignment to minority shareholders, landing on a standard, mixed stewardship call rather than a clear positive or negative. Capital allocation over five years mixed real per-share discipline — a 4.2% cut in shares outstanding and net debt held under the company's own 2x ceiling throughout — with a battery-materials and green-hydrogen bet that management committed to for three years, then wrote to zero and abandoned. Pay rewards capital efficiency and relative shareholder return, not size, and the long-term incentive's return-versus-peer leg paid zero for 2023-2025 despite a strong 2025 alone, showing real teeth. The biggest governance risk is structural rather than fraudulent: the Norwegian State's 34.3-34.5% stake is held for industrial-policy reasons (keeping head-office functions and technology in Norway), not for per-share value, capping shareholder-friendliness regardless of price. Verdict: standard / mixed stewardship — worth monitoring, not disqualifying.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Standard / mixed
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found no triggered disqualifier (no audit qualification, no pledging, no restatement, no enforcement on financial reporting, RPT well under 1% of revenue). The scan separately flagged a genuine extraction gap (the printed KPMG auditor's-report text, pp.225-230, missing from the pool's PDF text extraction) as a data-completeness item, not an inferred qualification — convergent secondary evidence (going-concern confirmation, zero qualification-language hits, unbroken KPMG tenure since 2010) supports an unqualified opinion.
- Management quality /100: **67**
- Capital allocation /100: **59**
- Incentive alignment /100: **75**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **55** *(capped — see Section 4)*
- Disclosure candor /100: **82**
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **55** *(floored — see Section 4)*
- Data quality /100: **78** *(from `00_governance-data-triage`: verdict "Sufficient," with two flagged sub-gaps — insider-transaction log absent; individual-executive pay deferred to a separate statutory report not in the local pool but obtained directly by agent 03)*
- Overall usefulness /100: **85**
- Insider ownership (one line): Board + Executive Leadership Team hold ~528,245 shares, ~0.027% of shares outstanding — a real but negligible personal stake; the dominant holder is the Norwegian State at 34.26-34.49%, held for industrial-policy reasons, not per-share value [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, §1].
- Biggest governance signal (one line): The Norwegian State's blocking-minority stake (34.26-34.49%) is held for an explicit industrial-policy mandate — "retaining a leading technology and industrial company with head office functions in Norway" — not per-share value maximization, and the Board has declined to even draft takeover-bid-handling principles because of it [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, §4; `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`, §4].
- **Governance Score /100** — `0.20×59 (CapAlloc) + 0.18×75 (Incentive) + 0.18×55 (ShFriendliness) + 0.16×82 (Candor) + 0.16×67 (MgmtQuality) + 0.12×(100−55) (GovRisk)` = 11.8 + 13.5 + 9.9 + 13.12 + 10.72 + 5.4 = **64.4**
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100** = 64.4 × (82/100) = **52.8**
- **Governance Rating:** Watchlist *(55-69 band)*
- **Confidence Score /100** (source quality): **82** — most findings rest on Tier-1 sources (audited annual report, KPMG-assured remuneration report, verbatim transcripts) at confidence 4-5; the lower-confidence items are the insider-transaction gap (confidence 1), the AGM vote-tally gap (confidence 1), and a handful of web-sourced peer-pay and departure-context data points (confidence 2).
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** 5 / 0

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** The Norwegian State's 34.26-34.49% stake is held for an explicit industrial-policy purpose, not per-share value, and the Board has formally declined to adopt takeover-bid-handling principles because of it — meaning a contested change of control, or state resistance to a value-accretive corporate action, is not a remote risk but a standing structural fact [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, finding 04-004; `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`, finding 05-016; RF-OWN-004].
- **Strongest bull point:** The incentive plan has demonstrated real consequences, not a rubber stamp — the LTI's TSR-versus-peer leg paid zero for the 2023-2025 period despite a strong stand-alone 2025 (Group ARoaCE 10.2%, TSR +26.9%) — and management owns its misses in plain language: it wrote "2025 EBITDA targets not reached; 2030 targets postponed" on its own scorecard, led a call with a fatal safety incident before financial content, and volunteered a negative free-cash-flow figure in the same breath as an EBITDA beat [`03_incentives-and-compensation.md`, finding 03-009; `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`, findings 06-001, 06-004, 06-005].
- **Single killer risk:** If commodity conditions stay weak and the State resists further capital return, M&A, or structural change because it conflicts with its jobs/HQ-retention mandate, minority holders have no realistic path to force change — the Board's own disclosure states a contested takeover is not on the table given the size of the State's stake, so persistent undervaluation could persist indefinitely rather than self-correct.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** The State's buyback participation was capped to hold its percentage stake exactly flat (34.26%) rather than let it drift up as other holders sold into the buyback, and the State's shares carry no preferential voting rights — both mitigate the "hold to loot/self-deal" version of this risk, leaving a "hold for strategic reasons, patient and non-extractive" risk rather than an active-expropriation one [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, §4].

## 3. Reconciliation

Two specialists disagreed on how to read minority-shareholder protection, and the disagreement is explicitly cross-referenced in their own reports. `05_board-and-shareholder-rights` read the mechanical architecture alone (one share/one vote, no poison pill, clean related-party transactions at ~0.27% of revenue, majority-independent board) as "adequate" protection, but explicitly deferred to `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior`'s parallel test of CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 6 (structurally unaligned controlling owner), stating that if Filter 6 tripped, the synthesis should treat the minority-protection read as "no better than weak/entrenched," superseding its own "adequate" conclusion. `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` independently did trip Filter 6 (RF-OWN-004): the Norwegian State's own Active Ownership Report states its rationale for holding 34.26-34.49% of Hydro is "retaining a leading technology and industrial company with head office functions in Norway" — an industrial-policy objective, not a per-share-value mandate. This synthesis takes the more conservative, reconciled view: the board-mechanics evidence (independent board, no dual class, no poison pill, clean RPT) is real and credited in the Shareholder Friendliness score, but it is capped at max 55 (per the Score Cap Rules) because the controlling shareholder's objective is not proven to be per-share value creation. Separately, this synthesis elevates `03_incentives-and-compensation`'s 26%-against say-on-pay finding to a formally tracked red flag (RF-SHR-001) under the Red-Flag Trigger Engine's "high AGM votes-against" criterion, even though the specialist agent itself did not assign a Red Flag ID to that finding — a more conservative treatment than the specialist's own. No other material disagreements exist between specialists; the Recycling/Extrusions growth-pillar miss, the battery/hydrogen washout, and the non-GAAP recurring-addback pattern are each corroborated consistently across the agents that touch them (01, 02, 06).

## 5. Stewardship Summary

This team has mostly delivered on what it controls and fallen short on what it does not: it met or beat its two headline, quantified financial targets (adjusted return on capital employed at 10.2% versus a 10% goal, and cost-improvement savings more than double its own internal estimate) while explicitly admitting a miss on its core downstream growth pillar (Recycling and Extrusions), and separately walked away from a battery-materials and green-hydrogen bet after writing the main investment to zero — a real, admitted destruction of growth capital, even though the balance sheet and buyback mechanics around it stayed disciplined (net debt never breached the company's own ceiling; the 4.2% cut in shares outstanding was a genuine reduction, not just an offset to dilution). Incentives point management at capital efficiency and relative shareholder return rather than scale — no revenue, EBITDA-size, or deal-count metric appears anywhere in the CEO's or CFO's pay plan, and the mechanism has already proven it can pay zero on its return-vs-peer leg for a full three-year underperformance window. Personal insider ownership is real but tiny (about 0.027% of the company combined for the whole Board and Executive Leadership Team), so the alignment case rests on the incentive design, not on skin in the game; minority shareholders get clean mechanical rights (one share, one vote, no takeover defenses, related-party transactions under 0.3% of revenue) but sit behind a Norwegian State shareholder whose own stated purpose for its 34%-plus stake is industrial policy, not per-share value — a structural fact the Board has cited as its reason for not even drafting takeover-bid principles. Management is candid in bad times by any concrete measure available here: it led an earnings call with a fatal workplace incident before discussing financial results, volunteered a negative free-cash-flow figure in the same sentence as an earnings beat, and never redefined a missed KPI to make it easier to hit — the one persistent soft spot is a "one-off" restructuring charge that has recurred and grown every year for three years, which is disclosed and audited but stretches the meaning of "one-off." The single most important reason to trust this team with shareholder capital is the incentive plan's demonstrated willingness to pay zero when three-year returns lag peers; the single most important reason for caution is that the largest shareholder is not trying to maximize what any of this delivers per share.

## 5B. Peer Governance Benchmark

`business-model/08_competitive-map.md` names three credible peers in Hydro's dominant Aluminium Metal segment — RUSAL (SEHK:486), Aluminum Corporation of China / Chalco (SEHK:2600), and Hindalco Industries (BSE:500440) — but that map supplies financial and competitive-position metrics (margins, leverage, scale), not the governance-specific metrics (board independence, insider/promoter holding, pledge, non-audit fee ratio, RPT intensity, AGM votes-against) this table requires, and no governance-focused peer dataset for those three names exists anywhere in the data pool. No peer set — relative governance not assessed on these specific metrics. Directionally, two of the three named peers (RUSAL, Chalco) are themselves state-linked or state-influenced, which suggests Hydro's government-ownership structure is not unusual for this sector globally, but this is an observation, not a benchmarked comparison.

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

- **State control (RF-OWN-004):** Has the Norwegian State ever voted against, or signaled opposition to, a value-accretive corporate action (buyback, divestiture, M&A) at Hydro? Is the industrial-policy mandate company-specific or a sector-wide pattern for Norwegian state holdings? Does it affect minority holders' ability to realize a re-rating, or only the probability of a takeover premium specifically?
- **Battery/hydrogen washout (RF-CAP-001):** Was this a one-off strategic misstep or does it reveal a recurring pattern of chasing adjacent-growth bets? Is the full cumulative capital committed (2021-2024) disclosed anywhere, and did the Board approve the entry and the exit with the same rigor? Is this material to the current valuation (sunk, not recurring) or a signal about future capital-allocation risk?
- **Dividend under-coverage (RF-CAP-003):** Was the FY2023-2024 shortfall a one-off payment-timing lag (cash paid in year N funding year N-1's declared dividend) or a genuine sustainability problem? Did net debt recovery in FY2025 resolve the issue, and has the variable-dividend formula been adjusted for lower prospective FCF?
- **Recurring "one-off" charges (RF-DISC-002):** Is the growing restructuring add-back (NOK 265m → 1,795m) driven by a one-time multi-year plant-closure program that should taper, or a structural cost problem being relabeled every year? Does it affect the credibility of adjusted EBITDA/EPS as a valuation anchor?
- **Say-on-pay opposition (RF-SHR-001):** Was the 26% opposition driven by the FY2024 pay rise (39%) against weak trailing TSR/EPS, or a broader complaint about disclosure (e.g., the undisclosed 25%-weighted individual-goal component)? Did the Board's LTI redesign (2027 grant) and simplified STI weighting (from 2025) actually address the proxy-advisor/shareholder concern, or just the symptom? What was the FY2025 report's own vote split at the 2026 AGM?

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standard / mixed | The State visibly supporting or voting for a value-accretive action against its own stated jobs/HQ mandate; a second consecutive year of reported (not just adjusted) RoaCE clearing the 10% cycle target; the AGM vote-tally data showing say-on-pay opposition narrowing materially after the LTI redesign; a multi-year (not single-bolt-on) M&A record with disclosed, delivered returns | A further debt-funded or reversed growth bet after the battery/hydrogen washout; the Alumetal CGU's thin (18%) impairment headroom actually triggering an impairment; a repeat or widening of the dividend-exceeds-FCF pattern; the recurring "one-off" restructuring charge continuing to grow in FY2026; any insider selling ahead of weak results once transaction data becomes available | Oslo Børs AGM vote-tally/scrutinizer results for the 2025 and 2026 meetings; VPS primary-insider (managers'-transactions) filings for the last 12 months; the FY2026 Alumetal CGU impairment test; a multi-year cumulative-capital figure for the battery/hydrogen bet |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- Stewardship verdict: **Standard / mixed** — competent on cost/return execution and disciplined on the balance sheet and buybacks, but with a real admitted capital-allocation misstep (battery/hydrogen washout) and a structural minority-alignment cap. Strongest supporting evidence: the LTI's TSR-vs-peer leg paid zero for three straight years of peer underperformance despite a strong 2025 — this is a pay mechanism that has already demonstrated real consequences, not a rubber stamp.
- Capital-allocation record: **mixed, not clearly value-creative.** Disciplined balance sheet (net debt never breached the company's own 2x ceiling) and a genuine 4.2% five-year reduction in shares outstanding are real per-share positives; offset by an admitted, fully written-off battery-materials/green-hydrogen bet and a bolt-on acquisition (Alumetal) now running on thin (18%) impairment headroom. Do not read this as a clean per-share compounding story.
- Incentive/ownership alignment: **incentives point at per-share value; personal ownership does not.** No revenue, EBITDA-size, or deal-count metric exists anywhere in the CEO's or CFO's pay plan — it is 100% RoaCE/TSR at the LTI level — but combined Board+ELT ownership is only ~0.027% of the company, so alignment rests on plan design, not on meaningful personal capital at risk. The dominant ownership fact is the Norwegian State's 34.26-34.49% stake, held for industrial policy, not return.
- Biggest governance risk / red flag: **RF-OWN-004 (structurally unaligned controlling owner), severity High.** The State's own Active Ownership Report states its rationale is retaining HQ/technology functions in Norway, not per-share value, and the Board has declined to adopt takeover-bid principles because of the size of that stake — a standing constraint on any market-driven correction of undervaluation. Four further High-severity flags exist (RF-CAP-001, RF-CAP-003, RF-DISC-002, RF-SHR-001); zero Critical flags.
- Hard disqualifier from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`: **None flagged.** Verbatim: "No hard disqualifier fires. The final verdict is not locked by this scan." A genuine extraction gap (the printed KPMG auditor's-report text, pp.225-230) was flagged as a data-completeness item, not an inferred qualification.
- §24 rejector filters: **Filter 6 (unaligned controlling owner) tripped** — RF-OWN-004, capping Shareholder friendliness at max 55 and flooring Governance risk at 55. Filters 1 (integrity), 2 (turnaround), and 4 (serial acquirer) did **not** trip — no adverse integrity signal was routed, this is not a turnaround thesis, and one bolt-on plus one reversed organic bet does not meet the serial-acquirer bar.
- Partial-data caps applied: Shareholder friendliness is capped (max 55, the RF-OWN-004 cap superseding the milder max-60 "no insider-transaction data" cap). No cap was applied to Incentive alignment despite the local pool's individual-executive pay gap, because agent 03 obtained the actual KPMG-assured statutory remuneration report directly from the company and used its real figures — flagged here as a provenance nuance for the master synthesizer to weigh, since it is a legitimate primary filing obtained outside the pre-loaded pool rather than a true "no disclosure" case.
- Biggest missing data point (single highest-value next request): **Oslo Børs AGM vote-tally / scrutinizer results for the May 2025 and May 2026 general meetings** — without them, the module cannot confirm whether opposition to pay, related-party items, or director elections is isolated to the known 26% say-on-pay dissent or is broader.
- **Explicit handoff:** This module supersedes `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`. The master synthesizer should treat THIS module's governance verdict (Standard / mixed), Governance Score (64.4/100, Confidence-Adjusted 52.8/100), and Governance Rating (Watchlist) as the primary governance read, not the business-model quick-read.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management has mostly kept its promises on cost and returns (beat its return-on-capital target and its savings target) but openly missed its main growth target and said so plainly.
- Capital allocation is a mixed bag: the company cut its share count and kept debt low, but it also poured money into a battery-materials bet for three years, then wrote it off to zero and walked away.
- Pay rewards return on capital and share-price performance versus peers, not just growing bigger — and the plan proved it has teeth by paying zero on its peer-comparison leg for three years running.
- Insiders barely own any stock personally (about 0.027% combined for the whole leadership team), so there is little of their own money riding on the outcome; the real controlling shareholder is the Norwegian government, which holds its 34%-plus stake to protect jobs and technology in Norway, not to make the stock go up.
- Minority shareholders get clean basic protections (one share, one vote, no anti-takeover defenses, tiny related-party dealings), but cannot realistically force a takeover or a big strategic change because of the size of the State's stake.
- Management is candid when things go badly — it led a call with a workplace death before talking numbers, and it admitted a negative cash-flow quarter and a missed growth target without spinning either — but it has relabeled a growing cost as "one-off" for three years running.
- No hard governance disqualifier was found (no fraud, no pledging, no auditor dispute, no restatement).
- This module is highly useful for the master synthesizer: it gives a full picture across management, capital allocation, pay, ownership, board, and candor, with only two narrow, clearly-flagged data gaps (insider-transaction log; AGM vote tallies).

RF-CAP-003,"Cash dividend paid exceeded that year's free cash flow in FY2023 (0.75x coverage) and FY2024 (0.36x coverage), coinciding with rising net debt in both years",High,"[Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab]",2026-07-18,"Dividend sustainability score component reduced -3 and -4 of 15 max in the two affected years","Confirm whether the variable-dividend formula was adjusted downward for FY2026 given lower recent FCF"

RF-SHR-001,"High votes against a key resolution — 26% of votes cast opposed the FY2024 remuneration report at the 2025 AGM, a materially high opposition level for a say-on-pay item",High,"[Norsk Hydro ASA Report on executive remuneration 2025, ""Dear reader,"" p.4]",2025-05-01,"Incentive-alignment pay-for-performance component reduced -3 of 10 max; elevated to a tracked red flag by this synthesis per the Red-Flag Trigger Engine even though the specialist agent did not assign a Red Flag ID","Obtain the FY2025 report's own AGM (2026-05-07) vote split once published; confirm whether opposition narrowed after the LTI redesign"

**Custom note on compensation:** the pool has aggregate FY2025 Executive Leadership Team pay (NOK 88,952k total) and Board fees (NOK 7,873k), plus the incentive metric itself (STI/LTI tied to Total Shareholder Return ≥12% performance target, share-based LTI with 3-year lock-in, no options). It does NOT have the individual named-executive breakdown (e.g., CEO-specific pay) — that lives in a separate "Norsk Hydro ASA Report on executive remuneration 2025" that the annual report references but which is absent from the pool. This is a real, material gap for agent 03's per-executive incentive-alignment read, even though the top-line metric and aggregate quantum are known. Recommend agent 03 flag CEO-specific pay as "Insufficient data — individual remuneration report not in pool" while still scoring the disclosed aggregate metric and TSR-linked LTI structure.

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** A proxy-equivalent (NUES Corporate Governance Report + Board's Report inside the Norwegian statutory Annual Report), aggregate compensation disclosure with a named incentive metric, a beneficial-ownership table (corroborated by a same-week Capital IQ cut), board composition/independence data, related-party disclosure, and a 5-year capital-allocation history (CIQ Financials workbook) are all present — so all six specialists can run, with two flagged sub-gaps (individual-executive pay detail; insider buy/sell transaction log) that narrow but do not block specific specialist reads.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record, capital allocation, incentives (aggregate/metric-level; individual-executive detail capped), ownership (beneficial-ownership level; insider-transaction component capped), board, candor
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — no disqualifier triggered. That scan separately flagged a genuine extraction gap (the printed Independent Auditor's Report text, pp.225-230, is missing from the pool's PDF extraction) as a data-completeness item to close, not an inferred qualification; convergent secondary evidence in the same filing supports an unqualified opinion.
- **Active partial-data caps:**
  - Shareholder friendliness capped at max 60 per MODULE_RULES "No ownership / insider-transaction data" row — the beneficial-ownership table is present and current, but no insider buy/sell transaction log (VPS primary-insider notifications / Oslo Børs managers'-transactions disclosures) exists anywhere in the pool.
  - Incentive alignment (agent 03) should treat CEO/named-executive individual pay as "Insufficient data" while still scoring the disclosed aggregate ELT total (NOK 88,952k FY2025), Board fees (NOK 7,873k), and the TSR ≥12% incentive metric — this is a narrower gap than a full "no compensation disclosure" cap and should not trigger the harsher max-50 cap, since real metrics and aggregate quantum are disclosed.
- **Critical missing items:**
  - Insider-transaction (buy/sell) data — no primary-insider notification log found.
  - Individual named-executive remuneration — the annual report explicitly defers this to a separate "Norsk Hydro ASA Report on executive remuneration 2025," which is not in the pool.
  - May 7, 2026 AGM voting/resolution results — the meeting had already occurred by this analysis date but its outcome (votes for/against the remuneration policy advisory vote, dividend approval, director elections) is not in the pool.
  - The printed Independent Auditor's Report text (pp.225-230 of the annual report) — an extraction gap already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, not a governance red flag on its own.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** the standalone "Norsk Hydro ASA Report on executive remuneration 2025" (the individual-executive compensation/incentive detail the annual report itself points to).

# Management & Track Record — NHY

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO | Eivind Kallevik | President & CEO since 2024 (effective 13 May 2024); employed at Hydro since 1998 | ~26 years at the company; ~2 years as CEO | CFO of Hydro 2013-2019, then EVP and head of Hydro Aluminium Metal 2019-2024, before becoming CEO. Succeeded Hilde Merete Aasheim, who chose to step down after five years as CEO and 17 years at Hydro [Web: Hydro.com news release, "2024: Eivind Kallevik new CEO," 2024-03-14 (unverified, dated)] | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Other details," p.46: "Eivind Kallevik... Employed in Hydro since 1998... Position since 2024... 111,605 shares"; Web: Hydro.com, 2024-03-14 (unverified)] |
| CFO | Trond Olaf Christophersen | CFO since 2024 (acting CFO from 6 March 2024, confirmed CFO effective 1 July 2024); employed at Hydro since 1997 | ~29 years at the company; ~2 years as CFO | Previously EVP Corporate Development at Hydro; stepped in as acting CFO after predecessor Pål Kildemo departed for a rival aluminium producer (Emirates Global Aluminium) [Web: Hydro.com news release, "Trond Olaf Christophersen appointed acting CFO in Hydro," 2024-03-06 (unverified, dated); MarketScreener, "Norsk Hydro ASA Appoints Trond Olaf Christophersen as CFO, Effective July 1, 2024" (unverified)] | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Other details," p.46: "Trond Olaf Christophersen... Employed in Hydro since 1997... Position since 2024... 23,659 shares"; Web sources above (unverified)] |
| Other — Chair | Rune Bjerke | Director since 17 June 2020; Chair since 7 May 2024 (Deputy Chair 2022-2024) | ~6 years on the Board; ~2 years as Chair | Former Group CEO of DNB Bank ASA (Norway's largest bank), Jan 2007-Sep 2019; earlier CEO of Hafslund ASA and Scancem International; concurrently Chair of Wallenius Wilhelmsen ASA and a director of several other Nordic companies | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Other details," p.46 (director-since/Chair-since dates, 26,700 shares); Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Board Members export (CIQ), "Bjerke, Rune" background text] |

The CEO is **not** a founder or owner-operator: Hydro's largest shareholder is the Norwegian State (34.5% of shares outstanding via the Ministry of Trade, Industry and Fisheries) [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1 Related party information, p.198], and Kallevik holds 111,605 shares — a career-employee stake accumulated over 26 years, not a controlling or founding position. Both the CEO and CFO are long-tenured internal promotions (Hydro "lifers"), which supports institutional continuity but means neither brings an outside perspective or a track record proven at another company at this level.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | Former CEO Hilde Merete Aasheim stepped down voluntarily in mid-2024 after 5 years as CEO / 17 years at Hydro (planned succession, not a forced exit — no evidence in the pool or web search of a dispute or performance-driven removal). Former CFO Pål Kildemo departed by 31 August 2024 to become CFO at Emirates Global Aluminium — a direct industry rival — which is a genuine competitive-departure signal worth watching, though nothing in the pool ties it to an accounting or governance dispute | [Web: Hydro.com, "Changes in Hydro's Executive Leadership Team," 2024-05-13 (unverified); Web: Yahoo Finance / GlobeNewswire, "Trond Olaf Christophersen appointed acting CFO," 2024-03-06 (unverified) — both dated web sources used only to confirm background/departure facts not in the pool] |
| CFO turnover | One CFO change in the last 3 years (Kildemo → Christophersen, 2024). Two years into the new CFO's tenure with no further change. Departure was to a competitor rather than a resignation "for personal reasons" with no destination disclosed — a mildly elevated flag versus a clean retirement, but not accompanied by any restatement, audit-qualification, or going-concern signal in the pool (confirmed by `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`: zero hits for "qualified opinion," "material uncertainty," or restatement language) | [Web sources above (unverified); business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md, disqualifier #1 and #5] |
| CEO succession / continuity | CEO, CFO, and Board Chair all changed in the same 12-month window (2024) — Kallevik (CEO), Christophersen (CFO), and Bjerke (Chair, promoted from Deputy Chair) all took their current roles within months of each other. All three are continuity appointments (internal promotion or existing director), not a clean-slate outside hire, which lowers — but does not eliminate — one-year concentration risk in institutional memory at the very top | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Other details," p.46; business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md, "Senior management turnover" row, severity 30/100] |

Frequent CFO turnover is not evident here (one change in 3 years, both predecessor and successor being long-tenured Hydro employees), but the concentration of CEO + CFO + Chair transitions inside a single calendar year (2024) is a genuine stability flag worth monitoring — it means the current top-three leadership group collectively has only about two years in their present roles, even though each individual has decades of tenure at the company.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Management says it is executing a "2030 strategy" built on four pillars: growing Recycling and Extrusions profitably, scaling renewable power generation, executing a decarbonization/technology roadmap, and shaping the low-carbon aluminium market through commercial partnerships [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Letter to stakeholders," p.4]. The explicit, checkable targets are: an adjusted return on average capital employed (ARoaCE — profit as a percentage of the money tied up in the business, adjusted for one-offs) of 10% over the industry cycle [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Profit," p.~44]; a cumulative NOK 6.5 billion in cost/procurement/commercial "improvement program" savings by 2030 against a 2024 baseline [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Profit," p.~44]; and net debt-to-adjusted-EBITDA held below 2.0x through the cycle [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Financial policy" section]. On top of these, the company disclosed capacity/volume targets for specific growth bets (e.g., 850kt of post-consumer scrap (PCS) recycling capacity, meeting "the lower-end target for 2030") and a CO2-reduction target (10% by 2025, later revised) [nhy-investor-day-2025.pdf, "2025 | Delivering on our key strategic priorities," p.6].

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |

| ARoaCE of 10% "over the course of a business cycle" | Standing target, restated FY2025 | FY2025 ARoaCE = 10.2%, above the 10% target for the year | Kept (single-year read; the target is explicitly cycle-average, so one strong year does not prove the multi-year average, but the latest print clears the bar) | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Letter to stakeholders," p.4: "adjusted return on average capital employed (ARoaCE) of 10.2 percent, above our target of 10 percent over the cycle"] |
| Improvement program: deliver savings "against the 2024 baseline" on the path to NOK 6.5bn by 2030; the specific 2025 estimate was NOK 0.6 billion | Program launched late 2024; year-1 (2025) internal estimate NOK 0.6bn | NOK 1.4 billion actually realized by year-end 2025 — more than double the internal estimate | Kept / beaten. Management's own words: "exceeding the estimate of NOK 0.6 billion for the year" | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Profit," p.~44-45: "By the end of 2025, Hydro realized NOK 1.4 billion in improvements, against the 2024 baseline, exceeding the estimate of NOK 0.6 billion for the year"] |

| CO2 reduction target: 10% by 2025 | Set earlier in the 2030 roadmap | 15% reduction achieved/projected by year-end 2025 — ahead of the 10% target | Kept / beaten | [nhy-investor-day-2025.pdf, p.6: "Projected to deliver CO2 reduction of 15% by year end 2025 vs target of 10%"] |
| FY2025 capital-expenditure guidance, original NOK 15 billion plan | Set earlier in FY2025 | Management itself cut the plan mid-year by NOK 1.5 billion "to adapt to market realities," and the investor-day scorecard reports a NOK 1.5bn reduction "vs original guiding" | Plan changed, not simply missed — management moved the goalposts downward in response to weaker markets and disclosed the change explicitly, rather than quietly underspending against an unrevised number | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Letter to stakeholders," p.4: "Midway through the year we reduced our capex guiding for 2025 by NOK 1.5 billion to adapt to market realities"; nhy-investor-day-2025.pdf, p.6] |
| FY2026 capex guidance (given at FQ4 2025 results, 2026-02-13): NOK 13.5bn total | Guidance issued Feb 2026 | Not yet due (FY2026 still open as of this report's 2026-07-19 as-of date); Street consensus (2026-07-15) currently models NOK 14,377m — about 6.5% above the guided figure, i.e. the market itself doubts the plan will hold, not evidence the company has missed it yet | Not yet assessable (forward-looking) | [earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md, §3: "Capital Expenditure (total)... NOK -13,500m... Street ~6.5% more capex spend than guided"] |
| Quarterly revenue vs Street consensus, last 4 reported quarters (FQ2 2025-FQ1 2026) | Ongoing | Revenue beat consensus in all 4 of the last 4 quarters (by +3.3%, +6.0%, +1.5%, +0.3% — a shrinking-then-modest margin); EPS was mixed (2 beats, 2 misses) over the same window, with the most recent quarter (FQ1 2026) posting the largest EPS beat of the four (+34.4%, filing-anchored) | Kept on revenue; mixed on EPS — no evidence of consistent sandbagging or consistent overpromising either way | [earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md, §6, Historical Beat/Miss Pattern table] |

Overall this is a **mixed, mostly-kept** record on the checkable items: the two headline financial/return targets (ARoaCE, improvement program) were met or beaten with a specific, quantified beat; the CO2 target was beaten; capex guidance was explicitly revised downward with the revision disclosed, not buried; but the core 2030-strategy growth pillar (Recycling and Extrusions) was missed on its own stated terms, and management said so in its own words rather than reframing the metric — a candor-supporting data point that section 6 of this module (`06_candor-and-disclosure-quality`) should weigh further.

The "improvement program" targets are internally generated, quantified, and reported against with an actual delivered number (NOK 1.4bn vs NOK 0.6bn estimate) rather than a resume-based promise | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Profit," p.~44-45] |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | None routed. The disqualifier scan found no triggered disqualifier and no adverse integrity signal of any kind — no pledging, no restatement, no enforcement action on financial reporting, no auditor dispute. The only unresolved item is a genuine extraction gap (6 missing pages of the printed KPMG auditor's report), explicitly flagged upstream as a data-completeness issue, not an integrity concern | [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md, §1 and §3] |

**Turnaround rule (Filter 2):** Not triggered. This is not a governance-turnaround thesis; no base-rate penalty or conviction cap applies under this filter.

**Integrity rule (Filter 1):** Not triggered. No adverse integrity "buzz" was routed to this agent from the disqualifier scan, and the scan's own review of pledging, related-party transactions, auditor tenure, and restatement/enforcement history found nothing adverse. `RF-MGT-005` is **not** emitted — there is no unresolved signal to carry.

## 5. Management Read

The top three leadership roles (CEO, CFO, Chair) all changed hands within the same 12-month window in 2024, but every one of those changes was a continuity appointment — a 26-year Hydro veteran and former CFO became CEO, a 29-year Hydro veteran became CFO after his predecessor left for a rival producer, and an existing director since 2020 became Chair — so this reads as a coordinated but orderly succession rather than instability, with roughly two years of track record in the new configuration so far. On the record itself: management met or beat its two headline, quantified targets (ARoaCE 10.2% vs a 10% goal; NOK 1.4bn of improvement-program savings vs an internal NOK 0.6bn estimate — more than double) and beat its CO2 target, while missing and explicitly admitting a miss on its core 2030 growth pillar for Recycling and Extrusions ("2025 EBITDA targets not reached; 2030 targets postponed," in management's own words at the November 2025 investor day). The single biggest management signal is that mix itself: this team hits its cost- and efficiency-side targets reliably but has twice fallen short on its stated downstream-growth ambitions and said so plainly rather than reframing the metric — a track record of disciplined execution on what it controls (cost, capital return) paired with a real, acknowledged shortfall on what it does not fully control (Recycling/Extrusions demand).

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — NHY

Reporting currency: Norwegian Krone (NOK million unless stated per-share, in NOK). Reporting standard: IFRS Accounting Standards as adopted by the EU, fiscal year ending 31 December [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.140]. Window covered: FY2021–FY2025 (5 fiscal years) plus the LTM Mar-2026 quarter where disclosed. This deepens and supersedes `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` per MODULE_RULES.md.

**Data-quality flag carried forward from `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`:** the Capital IQ workbook's own EBITDA/EBIT/"Return on Capital %" fields for NHY do not reconcile to the company's audited figures (a ~NOK 27bn gap on EBITDA), and the CIQ Cash-Flow tab's "Cash Acquisitions" and "Divestitures" lines read NOK 0 in every year shown — inconsistent with the disclosed FY2023 Alumetal acquisition and the 2024–2025 battery-portfolio divestments. This looks like a vendor template/classification mismatch for Hydro's statement structure, not a real economic zero. Per CLAUDE.md §4/§5, this report uses the company's own reported figures (EBIT after tax, Capital Employed, RoaCE, buyback/dividend cash flows, share counts) wherever the annual report discloses them, and does not use the CIQ EBIT, EBITDA, "Return on Capital %," "Cash Acquisitions" or "Divestitures" fields. The gap is flagged, not silently overridden.

## 1. Uses of Capital (5 years, FY2021–FY2025)

| Use | Cumulative Amount (NOK million) | Currency | % of 5-yr CFO (104,554) | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (net cash paid) | ~3,000–4,000 (estimate; see note) | NOK | ~3–4% | Not isolable from the CIQ Cash-Flow tab (reads NOK 0 in every year — a vendor extraction/classification mismatch, flagged above). Estimated from the disclosed deal price: Alumetal S.A. tender offer, aggregate equity price ~EUR 267m / enterprise value ~EUR 351m, completed 30-Jun-2023 [Web: mainsights.io / infrapppworld.com, "Norsk Hydro completes tender offer for Alumetal," 2023 (unverified)], converted at the 2023 average EUR/NOK rate of ~11.43 [Web: exchangerates.org.uk, EUR/NOK 2023 average (unverified)] — Inference, not from filings, and shown only as an order-of-magnitude cross-check to the filing-disclosed carrying value of NOK 2.7bn [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.7418 (impairment-test note)] |
| Buybacks (gross repurchase of common stock) | 5,946 | NOK | 5.7% | [Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock": 0 / 661 / 2,157 / 2,272 / 856 for FY2021–FY2025] |
| Buybacks (net of employee-plan share issuance) | 4,291 | NOK | 4.1% | Same source; issuance of common stock 51/48/568/964/24 for FY2021–FY2025 |
| Dividends (ordinary + special, cash paid) | 38,098 | NOK | 36.4% | [Same file, Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid" 2,822/7,098/11,501/5,015/4,581 + "Special Dividend Paid" 0/7,081/0/0/0, FY2021–FY2025] |
| Capex (absolute) | 54,399 | NOK | 52.0% | [Same file, Cash Flow tab, "Capital Expenditure": 6,020/9,604/13,638/13,555/11,582, FY2021–FY2025; cross-checked to `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1] |
| Debt repaid (gross) | 46,263 | NOK | 44.2% | [Same file, "Total Debt Repaid": 5,888/7,399/10,143/10,956/11,877] |
| Debt issued (gross) | 40,870 | NOK | 39.1% | [Same file, "Total Debt Issued": 4,293/8,963/9,242/4,727/13,645] |
| Debt (net repayment) | 5,393 | NOK | 5.2% | Debt repaid minus debt issued, five-year sum |

Five-year cumulative CFO (cash from operations) = NOK 104,554m (14,330+29,337+22,220+15,356+23,311) [same file, "Cash from Ops." row]. Capex (52.0% of CFO) and dividends (36.4% of CFO) dominate the five-year use of cash; buybacks are a distant third (5.7% gross); net acquisitions are the smallest disclosed use, though the true cash cost of the one material deal (Alumetal) cannot be pinned to a specific cash-flow line in this pool — a genuine data gap, flagged rather than assumed to be zero.

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Deal / Capital Commitment | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Alumetal S.A. (four recycling plants, Poland/Hungary) | 2023 (completed 30-Jun-2023) | ~EUR 267m equity / ~EUR 351m EV (web-sourced, unverified) — carrying value NOK 2.7bn per the FY2025 filing | Adds ~300,000 tonnes/year of post-consumer-scrap recycling capacity, contributing to the 850kt-by-2030 PCS target and the "profitable growth in Recycling and Extrusions" 2030 pillar [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, §3; integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, p.674] | **Not proven.** No deal-level return, IRR, or synergy-realization figure is disclosed. The FY2025 impairment test found the CGU's recoverable value exceeds carrying value by only ~18%, "as the coverage is limited, continued weak market conditions or further weakening of demand and/or margins is likely to result in impairment in a future period" — management's own words [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.7418–7426]. This is the single bolt-on deal in the review period; it sits inside the same "Recycling and Extrusions" growth pillar that `01_management-and-track-record.md` finding 01-008 records as an explicitly *missed* 2025 EBITDA target | One bolt-on deal, not a serial pattern — no divestiture was forced by this deal and it did not crowd out the core franchise. Opportunity cost is limited to the ~NOK 3–4bn of capital tied up in a business now one downturn away from a write-down | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.674, p.7418–7426; Web: mainsights.io (unverified, 2023)] |
| Battery materials & green hydrogen growth bet (Vianode equity stake + loan, Hydrovolt, Lithium de France, hydrogen development) | Invested roughly 2021–2024; abandoned Nov-2024 | Not separately quantified in this pool as a single cumulative figure; disclosed pieces include a NOK 375m loan to Vianode (impaired to zero) and a NOK 265m green-hydrogen impairment in FY2025 [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.7411–7413, p.10316] | Stated at the time as a strategic-growth-area bet ("battery materials and green hydrogen") | **Value-destructive, and management said so.** "In November 2024 Hydro announced that battery materials and green hydrogen will no longer be strategic growth areas for Hydro and that no further capital will be allocated." The Vianode ownership was fully written down in 2024 and the residual stake sold in 2025 "with no gain or loss" — a complete washout of that investment [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.6746–6749, p.10247–10248]. The FY2025 sale of the remaining battery-investment stakes generated a modest NOK 345m gain against the by-then-impaired book value — a recovery on the residual, not a recovery of the original capital [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.7964] | Capital and management attention were pulled toward an adjacent-growth bet that was reversed inside roughly three years; this is exactly the "geese sold to buy a hen" pattern CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 4 warns about, at small-but-real scale relative to Hydro's balance sheet | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.6746–6749, p.7411–7413, p.7964, p.10247–10248, p.10316] |
| Hydro Rein (49.9% renewables JV stake) | Finalized June 2024 | Not separately quantified as a cash figure in this pool | Deconsolidation into a 50.1%-owned equity-method JV with Macquarie Asset Management, framed as portfolio discipline rather than a forced sale | Neutral-to-mildly-negative: FY2024 and FY2025 results both carry impairment charges "in Rein... relates to investments involved in renewable energy production in Brazil" [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.10245], so the retained 50.1% stake is not problem-free even after partial monetization | Not a forced divestiture under deal pressure — read as ordinary portfolio management, not an M&A opportunity cost | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.575–576, p.10245] |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4):** Not triggered. One cash-funded bolt-on (Alumetal) plus one reversed organic-growth bet (batteries/hydrogen) over five years is not a serial-acquisition pattern, and neither deal was debt-funded near or above Hydro's own market value. No RF-CAP-004. That said, "no serial-acquirer pattern" is not the same as "good capital allocation" — the battery/hydrogen bet was a real, admitted value-destructive use of growth capital, and the Alumetal deal carries a disclosed, thin (18%) impairment-headroom risk that has not yet resolved either way.

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

Share price range table is the company's own disclosure [Integrated Annual Report 2025, "Common share data" table, p.64]. This module runs before valuation, so buyback prices are judged only against observable trading-range and disclosure evidence, not intrinsic value — "Intrinsic-value discipline not assessable from this module."

| Year | Amount Spent (NOK m) | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price (NOK) | Year's Trading Range (Low–High, avg) | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2022 | 661 | Not separately disclosed in this pool | Not determinable (exact shares repurchased for FY2022 not disclosed) | 51.49–89.95, avg 69.34 | Outstanding shares (net) fell modestly | No — issuance (NOK 48m, small) far smaller than repurchase | No specific in-year price disclosed | Cannot be judged for price discipline on hard evidence; small program relative to the year's swing | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.3006–3010, Cash Flow tab] |
| FY2023 | 2,157 | Not separately disclosed in this pool | Not determinable | 56.63–84.04, avg 68.85 | Outstanding shares (net) fell | No | No specific in-year price disclosed | Cannot be judged for price discipline on hard evidence | Same |
| FY2024 (Sept-2024 program, market purchases through 7-Jan-2025) | 2,272 (FY2024 cash) + spillover into Jan-2025 | 20,067,969 shares total under the program | **NOK 65.72/share** (NOK 1,319m ÷ 20,067,969 shares, company-disclosed total cost including transaction costs) | 53.24–75.10, avg 64.26 | Shares cancelled: 32,192,623 (issued-share count) between Dec-2023 and Dec-2024 | No — issuance NOK 964m vs repurchase NOK 2,272m; net reduction confirmed | Disclosed authorization band was NOK 20–150/share (statutory, not evidence of price discipline — the band is wide enough to be non-binding); actual execution price (NOK 65.72) sat just above the year's average trading price (64.26) and well below the year's high (75.10) | Roughly in line with the year's average — no evidence of buying near the high, but no evidence of exceptional bargain-buying either | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.8299–8337, p.3006] |
| FY2025 (completion of Sept-2024 program + Ministry pro-rata redemption) | 856 (FY2025 cash-flow figure) | 10,458,893 shares (Ministry redemption tranche) + remaining market-purchase completion | Ministry tranche: **NOK 65.60/share** (NOK 686m ÷ 10,458,893 shares, "at the same price terms as for the buybacks via the stock exchange") | 50.60–79.12, avg 64.59 | Shares cancelled: 30,526,862 (issued-share count) between Dec-2024 and Dec-2025; **no new buyback program was approved during 2025** | No | The Ministry's pro-rata redemption at the same price as the market buyback is a clean, non-preferential mechanic that kept the state's ownership share unchanged at 34.26% rather than letting it drift up as other holders sold into the buyback | Price sits close to the year's average (64.59) — again, no evidence of buying near the year's high (79.12) | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.2954–2957, p.8332–8337] |
| LTM (Mar-2026) | 686 | 10,458,893 (Ministry tranche, same transaction as above, timing straddles the LTM window) | NOK 65.60/share (same transaction) | N/A (quarterly, not annual) | — | No | Same as FY2025 row | Same as FY2025 row | [Cash Flow tab, LTM Mar-2026 column] |

**Net share count.** Total shares outstanding (net of treasury) fell from 2,051.5m (Dec-2021) to 1,965.3m (Dec-2025 and Q1-2026) — a 4.2% reduction over five years [Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Financials.xls, Balance Sheet tab, "Total Shares Out. on Filing Date"]. **Reconciliation flag:** the annual report's own "Common share data" table (p.64) shows a *higher* "Outstanding shares, year-end" figure for 2022–2024 (e.g., 2,068,998,276 for 2022) that does not fall in a straight line and appears to report gross *issued* shares rather than shares net of treasury for some years — it does not match the treasury-share mechanics disclosed in Note 7.6 (which nets out treasury shares acquired but not yet cancelled). This report uses the Balance Sheet tab's net-of-treasury figure, which is internally consistent with Note 7.6's cancellation mechanics, and flags rather than silently resolves the annual report table's inconsistency.

**Verdict on buyback discipline:** buybacks materially reduced net share count (not merely offsetting stock-based-comp dilution — employee-plan issuance was consistently small relative to repurchase spend) [Cash Flow tab]. Where the price is actually disclosed (the FY2024–2025 program), the company bought at NOK 65.6–65.7/share, close to that period's average trading price and meaningfully below the period's high — evidence of ordinary-course, not exceptional, price discipline. No evidence exists in this pool to judge whether FY2022–FY2023 buybacks were well-timed.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Dividend per share, FY2021–FY2025 | 6.85 / 5.65 / 2.50 / 2.25 / 3.00 (NOK; 2025 proposed, pending AGM 7-May-2026) | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, "Common share data" table, p.64] |
| Payout ratio (dividend / adjusted EPS), FY2021–FY2025 | 101% / 53% / 59% / 50% / 60% | Same source |
| Payout ratio, 5-year average (as disclosed) | 65% (per FY2025 report); 67% (per FY2024 report) — declining as the average window rolls forward | Same source |
| Dividend coverage by FCF (CFO − capex), FY2021 | 2.9x (cash div NOK 2,822m vs FCF NOK 8,310m) | Derived from Cash Flow tab |
| Dividend coverage by FCF, FY2022 | 1.4x (cash div incl. special NOK 14,179m vs FCF NOK 19,733m) | Derived |
| Dividend coverage by FCF, FY2023 | **0.75x — dividend paid exceeded FCF** (cash div NOK 11,501m vs FCF NOK 8,582m) | Derived |
| Dividend coverage by FCF, FY2024 | **0.36x — dividend paid far exceeded FCF** (cash div NOK 5,015m vs FCF NOK 1,801m) | Derived |
| Dividend coverage by FCF, FY2025 | 2.56x (cash div NOK 4,581m vs FCF NOK 11,729m) | Derived |
| Incremental ROIC (ΔEBIT-after-tax / ΔCapital Employed), FY2024→FY2025 | EBIT after tax fell NOK 10,377m→8,780m (Δ −1,597); Capital Employed fell NOK 123,428m→116,765m (Δ −6,663); ratio = **+24.0%, but not a clean incremental-returns read** — both the numerator and denominator shrank together (capital employed fell partly on the Tomago smelter full write-down and falling working-capital values as commodity prices eased, not on disciplined divestment of capital), so the ratio is a mechanical artifact of a shrinking base, not evidence of good returns on newly deployed capital | [Integrated Annual Report 2025, "EBIT after tax" / "Capital employed" table, p.10294–10313] |
| Reported RoaCE, FY2024 / FY2025 | 8.5% / 7.2% — **below** the company's own 10%-over-the-cycle target on a reported basis in both available years | Same source |
| Adjusted RoaCE, FY2024 / FY2025 | 8.5% / 10.2% — clears the 10% target only in FY2025, and only on the adjusted basis that strips out unrealized LME-derivative timing losses and impairments (a structural, recurring adjustment for Hydro, per `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §4) | Same source |
| Capex vs depreciation & amortization (growth intensity), FY2022–FY2025 | 1.12x / 1.46x / 1.34x / 1.13x — capex has run above D&A every year, i.e. net investment, not pure maintenance spend | [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, capex-intensity row] |

**Partial-data flag:** only two years (FY2024, FY2025) of company-disclosed EBIT-after-tax and Capital Employed are available in this pool, so the incremental-ROIC read above is a single-period comparison, not a multi-year series — cap conviction on that specific line accordingly. The five-year uses-of-capital, buyback, and dividend data (§1, §3, §4 payout rows) are not similarly limited.

**Dividend sustainability read:** the floor-plus-variable policy (NOK 1.25/share floor, up to 50% of adjusted net income variable) is structurally self-correcting — the variable component falls with adjusted earnings — but the *cash actually paid* exceeded that same year's FCF in two of the last five years (FY2023, FY2024), both high-capex years following a commodity-price-windfall year's large declared dividend. This is a real coverage gap (RF-CAP-003 threshold test), even though it reflects a payment-timing lag (cash paid in year N funds year N−1's declared dividend) rather than a chronically unsustainable payout — net debt did rise in both of those years (NOK 8.2bn FY2023 → NOK 16.0bn FY2024) before falling back in FY2025, and Adjusted net debt/EBITDA stayed within the company's own sub-2x target throughout (0.2x–0.9x).

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Capital has been allocated to create per-share value in some respects and to grow for its own sake in at least one clear, admitted instance. The best use of capital was debt discipline and the buyback mechanic: net debt never breached the company's own 2x adjusted-EBITDA ceiling even in the two years (FY2023–FY2024) when dividends outran free cash flow, and buybacks genuinely shrank the share count by 4.2% over five years rather than merely mopping up stock-based-comp dilution. The worst use of capital was the battery-materials and green-hydrogen growth bet (Vianode, Hydrovolt, Lithium de France): capital and strategic attention were committed to an adjacent-growth area for roughly three years, then explicitly abandoned in November 2024, with the Vianode stake written to zero and finally sold "with no gain or loss" — a clean value write-off that management itself narrated candidly. The single biggest capital-allocation signal is that this abandoned bet sits right alongside the one disclosed M&A deal (Alumetal) that is *also* running thin — an 18% impairment-test headroom the company itself flags as fragile to "continued weak market conditions" — so two of Hydro's three material capital-allocation bets over the period (Alumetal, batteries/hydrogen) are either unproven or already reversed, while the third (buybacks/dividends/debt) has been genuinely disciplined. Overall verdict: **mixed** — disciplined on the balance sheet and on returning cash, unproven-to-value-destructive on the growth side.

## Capital Allocation Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Organic reinvestment returns | 12 | 25 | Reported RoaCE below the 10% cycle target in both available years (7.2%/8.5%); adjusted RoaCE clears it only in FY2025; incremental ROIC only computable for a single, distorted, shrinking-base year [Findings 02-014, 02-015, 02-016] |
| M&A discipline | 11 | 20 | One bolt-on (Alumetal) with thin, disclosed 18% impairment headroom and no delivered-return figure; a separate battery-materials/green-hydrogen growth bet fully reversed and written off; no serial-acquirer pattern [Findings 02-004, 02-005, 02-006] |
| Buyback discipline | 13 | 20 | Net share count down 4.2% over 5 years, not merely offsetting SBC dilution; disclosed FY2024-25 program price (NOK 65.7) close to but not below the year's average trading price; state's pro-rata redemption executed on identical terms; no hard evidence available to judge FY2022-23 pricing [Findings 02-007, 02-008, 02-009, 02-010] |
| Dividend sustainability | 9 | 15 | Structurally self-correcting floor + variable policy, but cash dividends paid exceeded that year's FCF in FY2023 (0.75x) and FY2024 (0.36x), coinciding with rising net debt in both years, before recovering to 2.56x in FY2025 [Findings 02-011, 02-012, 02-013] |
| Balance-sheet prudence | 9 | 10 | Adjusted net debt/EBITDA stayed within 0.2x-0.9x throughout, never approaching the company's own 2x ceiling, even in the under-covered dividend years [Finding 02-017] |
| Per-share value creation | 5 | 10 | Net share-count reduction is real but earnings per share remain highly commodity-cycle-driven with no clean multi-year compounding trend [Finding 02-018] |
| **Total** | **59** | **100** | Mixed: disciplined balance sheet and buyback mechanics; unproven-to-value-destructive growth bets (Alumetal thin headroom, batteries/hydrogen washout); two years of dividend cash exceeding FCF |

No blanket partial-data cap applies to the whole scorecard — five years of uses-of-capital, buyback, and dividend data are available. The narrower incremental-ROIC sub-metric (Finding 02-015) is capped to a single-period read because only two years of company-disclosed EBIT-after-tax/Capital-Employed data exist in this pool.

## management-governance / 03_incentives-and-compensation.md

_Source: `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`_

# Incentives & Compensation — NHY

## 0. Data Provenance Note

Norsk Hydro's Integrated Annual Report 2025 (in `DATA_PATH`) discloses only aggregate Executive Leadership Team (ELT) pay [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1 Related party information, p.198] and repeatedly defers detail to a separate statutory document, the **"Norsk Hydro ASA Report on executive remuneration 2025"** — Norway's proxy-equivalent under section 6-16b of the Public Limited Liability Companies Act, subject to an advisory shareholder vote at the AGM [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report, "12. Remuneration of the executive management," p.297]. That report is **not included in `DATA_PATH`**. Per the partial-data rule, this agent's default would be to cap the read to high-level structure only. Instead — because the missing document is the exact primary filing named and cross-referenced throughout the in-pool annual report, is published by the company itself, is dated, and carries its own KPMG assurance opinion — this agent retrieved it directly from Hydro's official investor-relations site (hydro.com) to complete the read with individual CEO/CFO figures and full metric weights, rather than defaulting to "not assessable." It is cited below as a primary company filing (not as unverified web content), with page references to its own numbering. This is disclosed transparently so the synthesis layer can weigh the provenance; see §5 for the resulting score treatment.

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency: **NOK thousand**. Period: **FY2025** (year ended 31 December 2025); the report was published ahead of the AGM of 7 May 2026 and carries a KPMG limited-assurance opinion (ISAE 3000) dated 12 February 2026 confirming it meets the statutory disclosure requirement [Norsk Hydro ASA Report on executive remuneration 2025 ("Remuneration Report 2025"), Independent auditor's assurance report, p.30].

| Executive | Base | Annual Bonus (STI, earned) | Long-Term Incentive (LTI, earned) | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Eivind Kallevik, President & CEO | NOK 8,047k (+4.5% YoY, aligned with the Norwegian labour-union wage frame) | NOK 3,555k earned (max potential NOK 4,023k = 50% of base) | NOK 829k earned (= 10.3% of base; max potential 30% of base) — paid in Hydro shares with a 3-year lock-in, no options | Base + pension + STI = cash; LTI = mandatory share purchase, locked 3 years. Total comp NOK 15,060k: **71% fixed / 29% variable** | [Remuneration Report 2025, "Total remuneration" table, p.9; "STI overview," p.11; "Results for the CEO for 2025," p.20; "LTI results for 2025," p.22] |
| Trond Olaf Christophersen, EVP & CFO | NOK 4,598k | NOK 1,570k earned (max = 40% of base) | NOK 473k earned (= same 30%-of-base max LTI structure) | Cash + locked shares (LTI). Total comp NOK 8,910k: **77% fixed / 23% variable** | [Remuneration Report 2025, "Total remuneration" table, p.9] |

For context, 2024 comparatives: Kallevik total comp NOK 13,616k (71% fixed/29% variable, aggregating his part-year CFO and CEO roles); Christophersen NOK 8,458k (74%/26%) [Remuneration Report 2025, p.9]. Board directors receive **fixed fees only** — no performance-based element, no shares or options granted as pay (Chair Bjerke's total 2025 fee: NOK 1,167k) [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report "11. Remuneration of the Board of Directors," p.296; Remuneration Report 2025, "Remuneration of the Board of Directors," p.27].

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Annual bonus (STI) | Financial goal: Adjusted RoaCE (return on average capital employed, market-value-of-equity basis) vs. a 13% internal target (linear scale from 5%=50% payout to 13%=100% payout) | 35% (CEO/EVP Group staff); part of a 35%+40%+25%=100% CEO structure | **Returns-based** — a capital-efficiency metric (a return on the capital tied up in the business, similar to ROIC), not a size metric | [Remuneration Report 2025, "STI-plan," p.12; "Results for the CEO for 2025," p.20] |
| Annual bonus (STI) | Performance goals: 4 categories — People (safety), Planet (CO2), Partners (greener-product premium / customer / supplier), Profit (cost-improvement program vs. baseline) — 10% each for Group-level roles (CEO); Business-Area EVPs add BA-specific versions of the same 4 categories | 40% (CEO); 20% Group + 20% BA for BA-responsible EVPs | **Neither pure per-share nor size** — mostly safety/ESG/cost-efficiency KPIs; none of the four is revenue, absolute EBITDA, deal count, or headcount | [Remuneration Report 2025, "STI-plan," p.12-13; "Performance goals and achievement — Group," p.17] |
| Annual bonus (STI) | Individual goals: safety, leadership, execution on strategy (content "not disclosed in this report" as business-sensitive) | 25% | **Undisclosed content** — cannot classify; a genuine transparency gap on a quarter of the CEO's bonus | [Remuneration Report 2025, "Individual goals," p.13; "Individual target 2025: assessment of the CEO's results," p.20] |
| Long-term incentive (LTI) | 3-year average adjusted RoaCE (market-value basis) vs. a 10% internal target over the cycle | 50% | **Returns-based** (capital efficiency) | [Remuneration Report 2025, "LTI performance goals," p.21] |
| Long-term incentive (LTI) | 3-year Total Shareholder Return (TSR) relative to a named peer group (Nalco, Rusal, Alcoa, Century Aluminium, Hindalco, Chalco, Grupa Kety, Constellium, Kaiser, ProfilGruppen, Tredegar); payout band is Hydro's TSR minus peer TSR between −5pp and +10pp | 50% | **Returns-based** — TSR is by definition a per-share (price + dividend) return metric, and it is measured *relative to peers*, not on absolute scale | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "The Hydro share," p.64, footnote 4 (peer list); Remuneration Report 2025, "LTI performance goals," p.21-22] |

**No metric anywhere in the disclosed CEO/CFO plan is revenue, absolute EBITDA, deal count, or headcount.** The entire LTI (100% of weight) and over a third of the STI (35%) are pure capital-efficiency or shareholder-return metrics; the remaining STI weight (65%) is safety/ESG/cost-efficiency and an undisclosed individual component — not empire-building measures, but also not per-share value metrics. The LTI plan requires the earned amount to be invested in Hydro shares with a **3-year lock-in**, and **Hydro has no share-option scheme** — removing the asymmetric risk-taking incentive that options can create. The LTI is also conditional on the company posting a positive adjusted EBIT in the prior year (a loss-year payout blocker) [Remuneration Report 2025, "LTI performance goals," p.21; "Overview of remuneration elements," p.7].

**2025 LTI outcome shows real teeth, not a rubber stamp:** the RoaCE component paid 68.7% of its potential (3-year average market-value RoaCE of 6.9% — 5.46% in 2023, 6.9% in 2024, 8.3% in 2025 — against the 10% target), but the **TSR component paid zero**: Hydro's cumulative 2023–2025 TSR was +7.6% versus the named peer group's weighted average of +24.1%, a −16.5 percentage-point shortfall that falls below the −5pp minimum threshold for any payout. Blended, the 2025 LTI paid out at 34.3% of maximum potential — NOK 829k for the CEO, equal to 10.3% of his base salary against a 30%-of-base maximum [Remuneration Report 2025, "LTI results for 2025," p.22].

**Redesign in progress.** Following shareholder and proxy-advisor feedback on the FY2024 remuneration report (see §3), the Board is replacing the current backward-looking restricted-share LTI with a forward-looking performance-share plan from the 2027 grant, keeping the same 50%/50% RoaCE/TSR-vs-peer split but shifting the performance period to run *before* vesting rather than being locked in after a historical award — intended to tighten the link between pay and future, not past, shareholder value creation [Remuneration Report 2025, "Revised Long-Term Incentive (LTI) structure — Effective from 2027 grant," p.23].

**CEO's ESG/climate-linked pay is material and separately tracked.** Within the Performance-goal bucket, the report separately discloses that the CEO could earn up to 19.2% of his base salary on ESG-tagged sub-targets (environmental, nature, climate, social, governance, human-rights-in-supply-chain) in 2025 and actually earned 19.0% of base salary against them — a meaningful share of his roughly 44% of base salary in total STI, and distinct from the RoaCE/TSR financial metrics [Remuneration Report 2025, "CEO Compensation linked to ESG targets and achievements," p.21].

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest) | NOK 15,060k (FY2025), up 10.6% from NOK 13,616k in FY2024 | [Remuneration Report 2025, "Total remuneration," p.9] |
| Pay vs peers (if available) | FY2024 CEO total pay of ~NOK 14m sat well **below** the reported median of NOK 38m for other CEOs at Norway Metals & Mining companies with market caps above NOK 83bn — Hydro's own remuneration policy states pay should be "competitive but not market leading," and the outturn is consistent with that stated moderation, reinforced by Norwegian-government pay guidelines applicable given the State's 34.5% ownership | [Web: Simply Wall St, "This Is Why Shareholders May Want To Hold Back On A Pay Rise For Norsk Hydro ASA's (OB:NHY) CEO," dated ~2025 (unverified secondary source, but figures are drawn from the company's own FY2024 remuneration report); Remuneration Report 2025, "Remuneration structure," p.7 and "Dear reader," p.4 (Board Chair's own statement that "CEO compensation was competitive in Norway" but "behind European market levels")] |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | Mixed but with real consequences both ways: FY2024's 39% pay jump drew shareholder criticism because trailing 3-year TSR (2022-2024) was reported as roughly −4.3% and EPS had fallen ~14% over that window; FY2025's 10.6% pay rise came in a year of strong standalone results (Group ARoaCE 10.2% vs. 10% target, TSR +26.9%), yet the LTI's own 3-year TSR-vs-peer component still paid **zero** because the 2023-2025 3-year run underperformed peers by 16.5pp — the mechanism penalized the multi-year shortfall even as the single strong year lifted headline pay | [Web: Simply Wall St, dated ~2025 (unverified secondary, company-report-derived figures); Remuneration Report 2025, "STI results 2025," p.16; "LTI results for 2025," p.22; `management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md`, ARoaCE 10.2% vs 10% goal] |
| Say-on-pay support (%) | At the 2025 AGM, the Board's Report on Executive Remuneration for **FY2024** was subject to its statutory advisory vote: approximately **74% in favor / 26% against**. Hydro states it responded with a two-phase incentive redesign (simplified STI weighting from 2025; forward-looking LTI from the 2027 grant) in direct response to that feedback. The FY2025 report (this document) was itself put to an advisory vote at the AGM of 7 May 2026, where Hydro's own press release states all Board proposals on the agenda were adopted, but the specific vote split for this item is not disclosed in the sources reviewed | [Remuneration Report 2025, "Dear reader" and "Executive Remuneration Benchmark," p.4; Web: GlobeNewswire, "Norsk Hydro: Minutes from the Annual General Meeting 2026," 2026-05-07 (unverified, dated — confirms adoption but not the line-item vote split)] |

A **26% against** vote on a say-on-pay item is a materially high opposition level (most say-on-pay votes at large-cap companies clear with single-digit-to-low-teens opposition); it is a genuine minority-shareholder signal, not noise, and the Board's own letter treats it as such.

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

Hydro's pay plan rewards **capital efficiency and relative shareholder return, not scale**: RoaCE (a return-on-capital metric, split between the STI's 35%-weighted financial goal and half of the LTI) and TSR-versus-a-named-peer-group (the other half of the LTI) are the only two financial metrics in the CEO's and CFO's compensation, and neither revenue, absolute EBITDA, acquisition count, nor headcount appears anywhere in the disclosed structure — and the mechanism has real teeth, having paid zero on the LTI's TSR leg for 2023-2025 despite a strong 2025 on its own. The remaining ~65% of the STI (safety/ESG/cost-efficiency scorecards, plus an undisclosed 25% individual component) is not size-chasing either, but it dilutes the purity of the per-share signal and leaves a real disclosure gap on a quarter of annual bonus; combined with heavy fixed-pay weighting (71% for the CEO) and below-peer-median total pay that drew a notable 26% say-on-pay opposition vote on the prior year's report, the picture is one of a deliberately moderated, mostly-returns-based plan rather than an aggressive pay-for-growth one. **Conclusion: well-aligned (returns/per-share)**, with the caveats that the individual-goal weighting is opaque and the plan's teeth were demonstrated on the downside (a zero LTI payout leg) more convincingly than they have yet been tested on unchecked upside.

## Incentive Alignment Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Returns / per-share metrics | 18 | 25 | LTI is 100% RoaCE/TSR (returns-based); STI's financial goal (RoaCE) is 35% of the CEO's bonus; but the majority of STI weight (65%) is safety/ESG/individual, and returns-linked pay was only ~41% of the CEO's *earned* variable pay in FY2025 (Findings 03-004, 03-005, 03-007, 03-008) |
| Metric clarity and weights | 16 | 20 | Full numeric weights and thresholds disclosed for STI and LTI, with a linear payout formula for RoaCE and an explicit TSR band; the 25%-weighted individual-goal component is not disclosed (Findings 03-004 through 03-006, 03-011) |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | 14 | 20 | LTI's TSR leg paid zero for 3-year peer underperformance despite a strong FY2025 alone; but FY2024's 39% pay rise amid weak 3-year TSR/EPS drew a 26% say-on-pay opposition vote (Findings 03-009, 03-012, 03-013) |
| Long-term orientation | 12 | 15 | 3-year share lock-in, no options, EBIT-positive payout gate, and a shift to a forward-looking performance-share design from the 2027 grant (Findings 03-009, 03-014, 03-015) |
| Pay magnitude vs peers/performance | 8 | 10 | CEO total pay (~NOK 14-15m) sits below the disclosed Norway Metals & Mining peer median of NOK 38m; consistent with stated "not market leading" policy (Finding 03-013) |
| Minority-holder alignment | 7 | 10 | No dilutive options, real share ownership with claw-back provisions, and state-linked pay-moderation guidelines constrain excess; offset by the opacity on individual goals and the 26% say-on-pay opposition (Findings 03-006, 03-012, 03-015, 03-018) |
| **Total** | **75** | **100** | — |

Governance-Rating band for this score: 61-80 = Strong. No score-cap rule from the "no proxy/compensation disclosure" table is applied here because the actual proxy-equivalent document (the Remuneration Report 2025) was obtained and read in full, with individual figures and weights confirmed to the source's own page numbers (see §0).

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — NHY

## 1. Ownership

**Total insider (Board + Executive Leadership Team) ownership: ~528,245 shares, roughly 0.027% of the 1,965,278,330 shares outstanding at 31.12.2025** — a genuinely small personal stake in percentage terms, even though the CEO's 111,605 shares are worth roughly NOK 9.5m at the year-end NOK 78.2 close (real money for an individual, immaterial relative to the NOK ~167bn market cap). **Total government/state-linked ownership (Ministry 34.5% + Folketrygdfondet 7.3%) is ~41.8%** — the dominant force in the shareholder register. No individual insider or promoter holds a meaningful percentage stake; alignment with minority holders rests on the LTI lock-up mechanism and cash pay, not on large personal skin in the game.

Computed ratio: 0% pledged"] |
