**No pledging disclosed.** Norway/Oslo Børs has no promoter-pledge disclosure regime comparable to India's SAST framework, and none of the reviewed sources (annual report, CIQ Board Members export, Top Holders table) mention any pledge, lien, or encumbrance by the State or by any director/executive. This is a genuine clean read, not an unmonitored gap — the annual report, the disqualifier scan, and this agent's independent search all converge on zero hits.

## 4. Control Structure

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | None. "Hydro has one share class with equal rights for all shareholders" — one vote per share, no super-voting instrument, and the State's shares carry "no preferential voting rights." | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §4 "Equal treatment of shareholders," p.293; Note 9.1, p.198] |
| Controlled-company status | Not a formally "controlled company" under any single-majority test (no holder exceeds 50%), but the Ministry of Trade, Industry and Fisheries' 34.26–34.49% stake is a **blocking minority** under Norwegian company law (many special resolutions require a two-thirds majority of votes cast), and combined with Folketrygdfondet's 7.3% state-linked stake, state-affiliated holders control ~41.8% of the register — the single largest bloc by a wide margin over the next holder (BlackRock/Vanguard, each under 5%). | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1, p.198; NUES Report, p.293]; [NorskHydroASAOBNHY_PublicCompany.pdf, Top Holders, 2026-07-18] |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | The State has no charter-level board-nomination right disclosed; Board members are elected by the General Meeting via the Nomination Committee under ordinary process. However, the NUES Report explicitly carves out a Section-14 (Takeover) deviation "due to the Norwegian state['s]... ownership of 34.26 percent," meaning the Board has NOT adopted general takeover-defense principles because the State's blocking stake already functions as a de facto takeover deterrent — a real, if informal, control-rights effect of the stake's size. | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §14 "Takeovers," p.293: "The Board has not established general principles for handling takeover bids due to the Norwegian state['s]... ownership of 34.26 percent"] |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | **Government control test trips.** The Ministry describes its stake in Hydro via Norway's "Active Ownership Report" (Report to the Storting no. 6, 2022-2023) as held for "a long-term ownership perspective... for the purpose of retaining a leading technology and industrial company with head office functions in Norway" — an explicit industrial-policy / jobs-and-headquarters objective, not a stated per-share-value-maximization mandate. This is the textbook Filter 6(a) government-control conflict: the controlling shareholder pursues national-strategic objectives (keeping HQ functions and technology in Norway) that can diverge from a minority holder's pure return objective. | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §14, p.293: "the Active Ownership Report (Report to the Storting no. 6 (2022-2023))... expressed a long-term ownership perspective in the company for the purpose of retaining a leading technology and industrial company with head office functions in Norway... p.44"] |

**Unaligned-owner test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 6) — RF-OWN-004 triggered.** The Ministry of Trade, Industry and Fisheries' 34.26–34.49% stake is government control per Filter 6(a): the State's own disclosed rationale for holding the stake is industrial/employment policy (retaining head-office functions and technology in Norway), not per-share value creation. This is a structural cap on shareholder-friendliness, not a discount to arbitrage — a stock trading cheap under this ownership structure can stay cheap indefinitely because the controlling holder is not selling into strength, forcing capital discipline, or pushing for a re-rating; it is holding for national-strategic reasons. That said, two mitigants are disclosed and should not be discarded: (1) there is no preferential voting right and one share class, so the State cannot extract value through differential voting power; (2) the State's own buyback participation was capped to keep its percentage stake exactly flat (34.26%) rather than let its influence creep up via non-participation in a shrinking share count — a small positive signal that it is not using the buyback mechanism to increase control cheaply. Net: the government-control conflict is real and structural, but it is a "hold for strategic reasons" risk (patient, non-extractive) rather than a "hold to loot/self-deal" risk — Note 9.1's related-party transactions with the State-linked ecosystem (Statkraft/DNB/wind-JV power purchases) are individually small (~NOK 428m combined vs NOK 207,971m FY2025 revenue) and disclosed at arm's length. **Persistent cheapness under this ownership structure should be read by the valuation module as a possible value trap, not a margin of safety, until there is direct evidence the State would support or vote for value-accretive corporate actions (buybacks, M&A, structural change) against its stated jobs/HQ mandate.**

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Insider buys/sells vs subsequent results (timing) | Not assessable — no pool insider-transaction log exists to test timing against subsequent results. The one web-sourced data point (LTI/employee-plan purchases, April 2026) is a scheduled, plan-based transaction disclosed alongside routine annual-cycle compensation, not a discretionary trade that can be timed against results; it followed the Q1 2026 results release (29-Apr-2026 earnings call) by days, consistent with a standard post-results compensation cycle rather than opportunistic timing. | Insufficient Data (pool); [Web: GlobeNewswire, 2026-04-22/23 (unverified, context only)] |
| Unusual price / volume before announcements | Not assessable from the pool. The CIQ Public Company export shows a 52-week range of NOK 60.66–120.40 with the stock near NOK 85 as of 17-Jul-2026 (well off the 52-week high), but no pool source flags a specific pre-announcement volume spike or exchange inquiry. | [NorskHydroASAOBNHY_PublicCompany.pdf, Market Data, "as of Jul-17-2026"] |
| Exchange clarification sought for a price move | None found in the pool. No Oslo Børs announcement referencing an exchange-requested clarification is present in `data/NHY/`. | Insufficient Data |
| Institutional (comparable to FII/DII/MF) holding trend; any sharp high-quality-holder exit | Stable, high-quality register. Comparing the 31.12.2025 filing to the 18-Jul-2026 CIQ cut: the Ministry's stake ticked up slightly (34.26%→34.49%, likely a mechanical effect of the completed buyback-and-cancellation reducing the share count rather than fresh State buying — the State's absolute share count and percentage-of-issued-shares target were both held flat by design, per Note 9.1); BlackRock's aggregated position (combining its three separate FY2025 entities, ~4.7% combined, to a single 4.96% CIQ line) and Nordea's (2.6%→2.62%) and Storebrand's (2.1%→2.11%) stakes are essentially unchanged — no evidence of a high-quality institutional holder exiting. Vanguard (2.9% at 31.12.2025) does not appear in the CIQ top-5 cut, which lists only the top five holders by size — this is a table-truncation effect, not proven evidence Vanguard sold down, and should not be read as an exit without the full holder list. | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "The Hydro share," p.64]; [NorskHydroASAOBNHY_PublicCompany.pdf, Top Holders, 2026-07-18] |

No trade-timing red flag can be raised: RF-MKT-001/002 require pool evidence of insider selling ahead of weak results or an unusual pre-announcement price/volume move tied to a specific insider trade, and no such pool evidence exists here.

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Executives bought their stake through a Long-Term Incentive program that requires them to invest net proceeds in Hydro shares and hold for three years — a real purchase mechanism, not a pure grant — but the resulting personal holdings are tiny in percentage terms: the CEO's 111,605 shares and the CFO's 23,659 shares, plus the rest of the Board and Executive Leadership Team, add up to roughly 0.027% of the company, so there is essentially no insider ownership signal to read either way. There is no pool record of open-market insider buying or selling in the last 12 months (only a web-sourced, unverified, plan-based purchase in April 2026, which does not count as a red flag or a conviction signal under this module's rules), and no pledging by the State or any director is disclosed anywhere. The biggest structural fact for minority holders is not insider behavior — it is that the Norwegian state, through the Ministry of Trade, Industry and Fisheries, controls 34.26–34.49% of the company for an explicitly stated industrial-policy purpose (keeping head-office functions and technology in Norway), not for per-share value maximization; that single fact, not management's modest personal stake, is the dominant ownership risk this module can identify.

| Institutional-holder quality / trend | 8 | 10 | High-quality, stable register (BlackRock, Vanguard, Nordea, Storebrand, DNB, State Street, Folketrygdfondet); no evidence of a quality-holder exit between the 31.12.2025 filing and the 18-Jul-2026 CIQ cut [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, p.64; NorskHydroASAOBNHY_PublicCompany.pdf] |
| **Total** | **53** | **100** | Sum of scored components (Recent insider behavior excluded from the numerator as Insufficient Data, per MODULE_RULES partial-data cap — Shareholder friendliness capped at max 60 downstream in the `05`/`99` synthesis) |

## management-governance / 05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md

_Source: `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`_

# Board & Shareholder Rights — NHY

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | 11 members as of 31 Dec 2025: 7 shareholder-elected (all classed independent) + 4 employee-representative directors | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §8, p.295: "the Board had 11 members. Seven are elected by the General Meeting. Four are elected by employees in Norway"] |
| Independent directors (%) | 100% of the 7 shareholder-elected directors are deemed independent of executive management and material business relationships; counting the full 11-seat board (including the 4 employee-representative directors, who are not classed "independent" under the Norwegian code but are also not executives), the ratio is 7/11 = 63.6% | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §8, p.295: "All shareholder elected members were deemed to be independent under Norwegian standards in 2025"] |
| Chair / CEO split? | Yes, fully split. Chair: Rune Bjerke (non-executive, former Group CEO of DNB Bank ASA, director since 2020, Chair since May 2024). CEO: Eivind Kallevik (executive, in role since May 2024). The Chair is one of the shareholder-elected independent directors, not a company insider | [Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Board Members export, "Bjerke, Rune"; management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md, §1] |
| Audit / Comp / Nominating committee independence | **Audit Committee** (4 members: Marianne Wiinholt — Chair, Philip New, Espen Gundersen, Bjørn Petter Moxnes): 3 of 4 are independent shareholder-elected directors; the 4th, Moxnes, is an employee-representative director, and the filing itself footnotes that his participation is a reliance the company judges does not "adversely affect... the ability of the Board Audit Committee to act independently." **People & Remuneration Committee** (3 members: Rune Bjerke — Chair, Kristin Fejerskov Kragseth, Arve Baade): 2 of 3 independent, 1 employee-representative (Baade). **Nomination Committee**: elected by the General Meeting, independent of the Board and management by design, but the Norwegian state (Ministry of Trade, Industry and Fisheries) and the Government Pension Fund Norway (Folketrygdfondet) — Hydro's two largest shareholders — each hold a seat on it directly (Muriel Bjørseth Hansen for the Ministry, Karl Mathisen for Folketrygdfondet) | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §7 and §9, p.294-296] |

Both board committees that oversee financial integrity and executive pay (Audit; People & Remuneration) include one employee-representative director apiece. This is the standard Norwegian co-determination model, not a related-party conflict — employee representatives have no executive authority and no equity comp exposure of their own — but it means these two committees are not 100% independent-director bodies in the stricter sense used in some other regimes (e.g., the US SEC's fully-independent audit-committee requirement).

## 2. Tenure & Refreshment

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Average director tenure | Shareholder-elected directors average ~4.1 years on the Board (Bjerke 6 yrs since 2020; Kragseth 4 yrs since 2022; Wiinholt 10 yrs since 2016; Gundersen 2 yrs since 2024; New 4 yrs since 2022; Toogood 2 yrs since 2024; Wahl 1 yr since 2025) | [Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Board Members export, individual "Years on Board" fields] |
| Overboarding / staleness signs | Marianne Wiinholt, Chair of the Audit Committee, is the longest-tenured shareholder-elected director at 10 years (since 2016) — long enough that some codes (e.g., the UK Corporate Governance Code's 9-year guideline) would flag a fresh independence review, though the Norwegian NUES code carries no such numeric tenure trigger and the company states she remains independent. Separately, two directors carry a meaningfully heavier concurrent board load: Kim Wahl sits on ~5 other boards/foundations (Aspelin Ramm Gruppen, Fristads Kansas, Kavli Holding, Vend Marketplaces, Voxtra Foundation) alongside Hydro, and Espen Gundersen sits on 4 other listed/private boards (Hexagon Purus, Kid ASA — as Chair, Scatec ASA, Compac Sorting Equipment) alongside Hydro and his Hydro Audit Committee seat | [Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Board Members export, "Other Boards" fields for Wahl and Gundersen] |
| Recent refreshment | 3 of the 7 shareholder-elected directors joined within the last 2 years: Espen Gundersen and Jane Toogood (both May 2024) and Kim Wahl (May 2025) — a genuine one-third-plus refresh of the shareholder-elected bench in two AGM cycles | [Norsk Hydro ASA OB NHY Board Members export; integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §8] |

Refreshment is real (three new shareholder-elected directors in two years), which offsets the single long-tenured Audit Committee chair. The overboarding signal on Wahl and Gundersen is a monitoring item, not disqualifying — most of their other seats are private-company or foundation boards with lighter workloads than a second large-cap listed board, but Gundersen also chairs Kid ASA, which adds meaningful time commitment on top of his Hydro Audit Committee seat.

## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

| RPT Type | Amount | % of Revenue | % of PAT | % of Net Worth | Arm's Length? | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Power purchases from Hydro Rein-related JV entities (Sweden wind, Brazil wind) | NOK 428m (NOK 64m + NOK 42m Sweden, NOK 322m Brazil, FY2025) | 0.21% | 6.4% | 0.4% | Yes — "at fixed prices subject to inflation adjustment" under long-term power purchase agreements; company states "transactions with related parties are at arm's length principles" | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1, p.198] |
| Rent and related services paid to Norsk Hydros Pensjonskasse (Hydro's own pension trust) | NOK 98m (FY2025) vs NOK 94m (FY2024) | 0.05% | 1.5% | 0.09% | Yes — priced on 2006/2015 market-benchmarked rates, per a rental arrangement whose current term expires Feb 2027 | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1, p.198] |
| Electricity sold to the pension trust / administrative-cost reimbursements to/from the pension trust | NOK 6m electricity sale + ~NOK 26m combined admin/service reimbursements (FY2025) | 0.02% | 0.5% | 0.03% | Yes — services at cost reimbursement, standard trust-administration arrangement | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1, p.198] |
| Norwegian State as related party (34.5% shareholder, Ministry of Trade, Industry and Fisheries) — general business dealings with state-controlled entities (e.g., power from Statkraft, banking from DNB) | Not separately quantified; Board states these are "agreed independently of the possible control exercised by the State" | Not quantified | Not quantified | Not quantified | Company-asserted arm's length; no preferential pricing or terms disclosed | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1, p.198] |
| Outstanding receivables from related parties (pension trust) | NOK 4m receivable / NOK 6m payable to the pension trust, both settled early 2026 | Immaterial | Immaterial | Immaterial | Yes | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, Note 9.1, p.198] |

Total disclosed related-party flows sum to roughly NOK 558 million against FY2025 revenue of NOK 207,971 million — about **0.27% of revenue**, well inside the Low materiality band (<1% of revenue/assets) under the module's thresholds. The Board explicitly states: "no material transactions occurred in 2025 between the group and its shareholders, Board members, Executive Leadership Team or related parties" beyond the items itemized above [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §9, p.296]. These RPTs are recurring, small, and disclosed as arm's length; `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` independently confirmed the >25%-of-revenue/expenses hard disqualifier does not trigger (computed ratio well under 1%). Hydro's joint-venture partners (e.g., Qatalum, 50% joint venture with Qatar Petroleum) are explicitly **not** treated as related parties — the company classifies them as competitors in other dealings, a reasonable IFRS-consistent distinction, though it is worth noting as the one judgment call in the RPT boundary. No promoter-style leakage channel exists in this ownership structure: there is no controlling family or promoter group extracting royalties, management fees, or rent — the largest shareholder is a state ministry with a disclosed hands-off policy on day-to-day operations.

**Read:** No material related-party leakage to a controlling insider. This is a structural strength relative to promoter-controlled peers in other jurisdictions.

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill | No | "Hydro's Articles of Association and underlying governing documents contain no defense mechanisms against acquisition offers, and no measures have been implemented to restrict the acquisition of shares in the company" | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §14, p.297] |
| Classified / staggered board | Not disclosed as staggered | Shareholder-elected directors serve terms of up to two years, but the NUES Report describes no staggered-class structure (all shareholder-elected seats appear subject to periodic re-election as a group, not split into rotating classes) | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §8, p.296] |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | No | "Hydro has one share class with equal rights for all shareholders" and "there are no different voting rights associated with the shares outstanding"; the Norwegian State's 34.5% stake carries "no preferential voting rights" | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §4-5, p.294; Note 9.1, p.198] |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Not disclosed in the pool | The filing does not state whether director elections use a majority-of-votes-cast standard versus plurality; this is a genuine data gap for this specific mechanic | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §6, p.294-295 — general voting process described, standard not specified] |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Partial disclosure | The filing confirms shareholders may submit written proposals for agenda items/resolutions at least four weeks before the ordinary General Meeting. It does not restate the statutory Norwegian Companies Act threshold for shareholders to demand an extraordinary general meeting; that is a matter of Norwegian company law generally, not company-specific disclosure in this filing — **not confirmed from this pool, treated as a data gap rather than assumed** | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §6, p.294] |

**One deviation worth flagging on its own:** the Board has explicitly declined to adopt the NUES-recommended practice of establishing "general principles for handling takeover bids," and states this is specifically **because of the Norwegian state's 34.26% ownership stake** — implying the Board itself judges a contested takeover to be impractical given the state's blocking position, rather than because Hydro has installed its own defenses [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §14, p.297: "The Board has not established general principles for handling takeover bids due to the Norwegian state... ownership of 34.26 percent of the Hydro shares"]. This means the practical takeover-defense mechanism at Hydro is not a poison pill or staggered board — it is the sheer size of the state's stake, which also functions as a blocking minority (above the roughly one-third threshold) against special resolutions requiring a two-thirds majority under Norwegian company law (e.g., mergers, capital-structure changes, Articles amendments). This is disclosed, not hidden, but it is a real structural constraint on any change-of-control outcome that the state's Ministry does not support.

## 4A. Shareholder Voting & Dilution

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| AGM votes against (remuneration / RPT / director / auditor resolutions) | **Not available in the pool.** The May 9, 2025 AGM saw 80.02% of voting share capital represented, but no vote-tally or scrutinizer breakdown (for/against/abstain on any specific resolution) is disclosed in the annual report or elsewhere in the data pool. The May 7, 2026 AGM (covering the FY2025 dividend proposal and a management-remuneration-report advisory vote) had already occurred by this report's 2026-07-19 as-of date, but its results are likewise absent from the pool | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "The Hydro share," p.2959: "A total of 80.02 percent of the voting share capital was represented"; management-governance/00_governance-data-triage.md, row "AGM voting"] |
| Proxy-advisor recommendations (ISS / Glass Lewis / IiAS) | Not found in the pool | No proxy-advisor report or recommendation for any Hydro resolution is in the data pool | — |
| Institutional opposition to any resolution | Not assessable | No voting breakdown by holder class is available | — |
| Dilution history (preferential allotment, warrants, QIP, ESOP pool) | Net share count has **fallen** every year: from 2,051m shares (Dec-2021) to 1,965m shares (Dec-2025/Q1-2026), driven by buybacks (NOK 661m/2,157m/2,272m/856m/686m, 2022-2026 LTM) against only token employee-plan issuance (NOK 24-964m/yr); the 2024 buyback program's shares (including the state's pro-rata portion) were cancelled at the May 2025 AGM. The company has no share-option scheme; its long-term incentive plan is share-based with a 3-year lock-in | [business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md, "Net share count trajectory" row; integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, NUES Report §12, p.297: "Hydro has no share option scheme"] |

**Read:** dilution risk is low and the share count trend favors existing holders, but the total absence of AGM vote-tally data is a real transparency gap for this specific test — the module cannot confirm whether any resolution (director election, remuneration policy, auditor reappointment) drew meaningful shareholder opposition in 2025 or 2026. This caps the "AGM opposition / minority protection" score component below (§ score table) rather than being scored on assumed clean data.

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

The architecture is genuinely shareholder-friendly on paper: one share class with equal voting rights, no poison pill, no staggered board, no preferential voting for the state, a fully split Chair/CEO structure, a majority-independent shareholder-elected board bench that has refreshed three of seven seats in the last two years, and related-party dealings that are small (about 0.27% of revenue), recurring, and explicitly attested as arm's length by the Board — none of the promoter-style leakage risk that shows up in controlled-company structures elsewhere applies here. The single biggest structural risk to minority holders is not board entrenchment in the usual sense — it is that the Norwegian state's ~34.3-34.5% stake is large enough to function as a blocking minority over major corporate-law resolutions and large enough that Hydro's own Board has declined to even draft takeover-bid-handling principles, citing the state's position as the reason a contested change of control is not realistically on the table; the state's stated rationale for holding the stake ("retaining a leading technology and industrial company with head office functions in Norway," per the Storting Active Ownership Report) is a national-strategic objective, not a per-share-value mandate, and that gap between the controlling shareholder's goal and a per-share-value goal is the item `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` should test directly against CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 6 (structurally unaligned controlling owner) — this agent flags the fact pattern but does not itself apply that filter's score cap, since `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` cross-module input is not available at time of this report (parallel layer, not yet run). A second, more contained gap is that the pool contains no AGM vote-tally data for either the 2025 or 2026 meeting, so opposition levels on pay, RPT, or director resolutions cannot be checked. On the evidence actually in hand, this reads as **adequate** protection: the mechanical rights (one share/one vote, no defenses, clean RPT, independent-majority board) are real and verified, but the concentration of a strategically-motivated 34%+ shareholder and the missing voting-transparency data mean the read stops short of "strong."

*Cross-module note for `99_management-governance-synthesis`:* if `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` independently concludes the Norwegian state's stake and stated national-strategic mandate meet the RF-OWN-004 bar (structurally unaligned controlling owner, CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 6), the synthesis should apply the associated Shareholder friendliness cap (max 55) and Governance risk floor (55) per the Score Cap Rules, and treat this module's minority-protection read as no better than "weak / entrenched" on that basis, superseding the "adequate" conclusion reached here on the board-mechanics evidence alone.

Partial-data note: the AGM-voting gap (Finding 05-020) is a genuine, disclosed absence — not a language issue and not company-side opacity, since Norwegian AGM results are normally published via Oslo Børs stock-exchange notice; the notice itself is simply not present in this data pool. This caps the "AGM opposition / minority protection" component but does not trigger the module's "no board disclosure" partial-data rule, since board composition, independence, and committee data are all present and Green-rated.

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — NHY

Norsk Hydro ASA reports under IFRS Accounting Standards as adopted by the EU, in Norwegian Krone (NOK), fiscal year ending 31 December [Integrated Annual Report 2025, p.140]. This agent uses two **verbatim** earnings-call transcripts in the data pool — the FQ4 2025 call (2026-02-13) and the FQ1 2026 call (2026-04-29), both S&P Global Market Intelligence-transcribed records of management's own words and analyst Q&A — so the partial-data cap for "no transcripts" does NOT apply here. `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` is present and used as the non-GAAP cross-check, so that cap also does not apply.

## 1. Owning the Misses

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2025 Recycling/Extrusions EBITDA growth-pillar targets not reached; some 2030 targets postponed | Stated in management's own words on the investor-day scorecard slide ("2025 EBITDA targets not reached; 2030 targets postponed") and repeated in the CEO's annual-report letter ("challenges made us fall short of the 2025 targets, and we have postponed some of the 2030 targets") — the metric itself was not redefined or dropped after the miss | **Owned** — plain language, no reframing of the KPI | [nhy-investor-day-2025.pdf, p.6; integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Letter to stakeholders," p.5] |
| FY2025 capex guidance cut mid-year by NOK 1.5bn vs the original NOK 15bn plan | Disclosed explicitly in the CEO's own letter as a deliberate response ("we reduced our capex guiding for 2025 by NOK 1.5 billion to adapt to market realities"), not a silent underspend against an unrevised number | **Owned** — the revision itself, and its cause, are named | [integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, "Letter to stakeholders," p.4] |
| FQ4 2025: reported EBITDA of ~NOK 2bn vs adjusted EBITDA of NOK 5.6bn; reported net loss of NOK (2.2)bn vs adjusted net income of NOK 1.7bn — the weakest reported quarter in the pool | CFO walked through every adjusting item live on the call with exact NOK figures (unrealized LME derivative loss NOK 2.3bn, rationalization/closure costs NOK 1.3bn, impairments NOK 700m) rather than citing only the adjusted headline. The CEO's opening remarks also named the "difficult but necessary restructuring decisions" (workforce reduction, 5 extrusion-plant closures) driving the quarter, without euphemism-only framing | **Owned** — the reported (GAAP) loss and its exact drivers were stated, not skipped past in favor of the adjusted number alone | [Norsk Hydro ASA, Q4 2025 Earnings Call transcript, 2026-02-13, Presentation section, CFO remarks] |
| Q1 2026: negative free cash flow of NOK (4)bn despite a strong adjusted EBITDA beat | The CEO led his own highlights paragraph with it — "operating capital is also higher, resulting in a negative free cash flow of NOK 4 billion" — placed in the same breath as the EBITDA beat, not omitted or pushed to a later slide | **Owned** — negative cash flow was volunteered in the opening highlights, not buried | [Norsk Hydro ASA, Q1 2026 Earnings Call transcript, 2026-04-29, Presentation section] |
| Fatal contractor incident at the Alunorte refinery (30 March 2026) | CEO opened the Q1 2026 call with it directly, before any financial content: "we are deeply saddened by the passing of a contractor... if the investigation concludes that inadequate safety measures contributed to this incident, we will, of course, take this extremely seriously." No minimization, no deflection to "the investigation will clarify" as a way to avoid commitment | **Owned** — led the call, plain acknowledgment of possible fault pending investigation | [Norsk Hydro ASA, Q1 2026 Earnings Call transcript, 2026-04-29, Presentation section] |
| Consensus EPS misses in FQ3 2025 (−12.8%) and FQ4 2025 (−6.8%) | No formal group EPS guidance exists to miss (confirmed in `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §2), so this is a Street-consensus miss, not a broken company promise; the underlying drivers (lower alumina prices, extrusion headwinds, restructuring costs) were explained in full on both calls with itemized bridges, not glossed over | **Owned** at the level of explaining the drivers — but not a "promise miss" since no formal EPS target existed to break | [earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md, §6; Q4 2025 and Q3 2025 transcript presentation sections] |

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | "Significant rationalization and closure costs" have been added back to EBITDA/EBIT every year for three straight years and the amount is growing, not shrinking: NOK 265m (FY2023) → NOK 407m (FY2024) → NOK 1,795m (FY2025). A goodwill/PP&E impairment has also been booked every year (NOK 4,424m / 22m / 1,069m). The module's own earnings-quality rule treats a charge that recurs every period as not genuinely one-off | earnings/06, §4–§5, §8 (Accounting Trap Checklist, severity 60/100 for recurring restructuring) |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | The gap is modest and sign-flipping at the EBITDA line (−4.4% FY2023, −0.8% FY2024, +12.4% FY2025) but large and **directionally consistent every year** at net income/EPS: reported net income sat below the company's own adjusted figure by +34% (FY2025), +84% (FY2024), and +180% (FY2023). This is the single largest reported-vs-adjusted divergence earnings/06 identifies for Hydro | earnings/06, §7 (Reported vs Adjusted Reconciliation), §10 |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | Partially. At EBITDA, the adjusted figure is a fair operating read (the swings are structural, non-cash derivative-timing effects). At net income and EPS, the adjusted figure is persistently and materially higher than reported, driven by impairments and the recurring rationalization add-back — a reader who anchors on adjusted EPS alone (e.g. NOK 4.26 adjusted vs NOK 1.77 reported in FY2023) gets a rosier read of that year than the audited bottom line supports. The company discloses the full reconciliation and it is audited — this is a disclosed-but-persistent framing bias, not concealment, and earnings/06 scored overall earnings quality 55/100 ("mixed... material concerns from recurring one-off charges") on this exact pattern | earnings/06, §7, §9, §10 |

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Segment-level disclosure is genuinely granular: each quarter management gives a five-segment (Bauxite & Alumina, Aluminium Metal, Metal Markets, Extrusions, Energy) forward bridge with quantified NOK/USD cost and price ranges — e.g. the FQ1 2026 call gave specific figures such as "67% of Q2 primary production booked at USD 3,000/tonne," "carbon costs expected to increase NOK 150-250 million," and "fixed costs NOK 300-400 million higher" — well beyond boilerplate segment commentary [Q1 2026 transcript, presentation section]. One disclosure gap has persisted rather than improved: Hydro still does not split maintenance ("sustaining") capex from growth capex in the data pool, despite qualitatively flagging "historically high sustaining capital expenditure," which forces this module's free-cash-flow read to use total capex as a blunter proxy [earnings/06, §1, footnote 3; integrated-annual-report-2025.pdf, p.51]. No evidence was found of any KPI or segment disclosure becoming *less* transparent over time — if anything the quarter-over-quarter cost/price bridges have become more numerically specific across the two transcripts reviewed.

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

The worst reported quarter in the pool is FQ4 2025 (reported net loss of NOK (2.2)bn, EPS consensus miss of −6.8%, negative Extrusions EBITDA, plant closures and a workforce reduction). The CEO's tone was matter-of-fact rather than promotional — he named the restructuring as "difficult but necessary" and did not use language claiming strength where the numbers showed weakness. In Q&A, analysts asked pointed, specific questions (extrusion order-book visibility for H2 2026, whether the strong B&A trading result was sustainable, the drivers of the fixed-cost increase) and received direct numeric answers each time (e.g., "around NOK 300 million in very strong commercial results in B&A in Q4") rather than deflection to "we'll follow up offline." The one genuinely evasive-sounding answer — on whether extrusion volumes would recover in H2 2026 — was an honest "too early to conclude" rather than a confident but unsupported forecast, which is itself a candor-consistent response to real forward uncertainty rather than spin.

## 5. Candor Read

Management owns its misses in plain language: it wrote "2025 EBITDA targets not reached; 2030 targets postponed" on its own investor-day slide, led its Q1 2026 highlights with a negative free-cash-flow number rather than hiding it behind an EBITDA beat, and opened a call with a fatal workplace incident before any financial content. Q&A across both verbatim transcripts shows analysts getting specific, numeric answers rather than deflection. The one real soft spot is non-GAAP discipline — a "rationalization and closure cost" that has been added back as non-recurring every year for three years, growing each time, and a net-income/EPS gap versus the adjusted figure that has run from +34% to +180% across FY2023–FY2025 — fully disclosed and audited, but a persistent pattern that flatters the adjusted bottom line versus the reported one. No unresolved adverse integrity signal was routed from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (confirmed in `01_management-and-track-record.md`, §4A), so there is no compounding integrity concern to weigh against this read. On balance: **candid / high-trust**, with the qualifier that reported net income/EPS should never be read standalone given the scale and persistence of the adjusted-vs-reported gap.
